| Giovanni Odaglia | ABU DHABI HOSPITALITY ACADEMY - LES ROCHES | CEO |  | giovanni.odaglia@lesroches.edu |  | Leadership | Public campus momentum - 2026 open days and new-campus introduction | Les Roches Abu Dhabi's next graduation ceremony | Hi Giovanni,

I was looking at Les Roches' Abu Dhabi campus page and the 2026 open-day calendar. The Academy is presenting a very events-facing hospitality experience in Zayed Sports City, so graduation and recognition moments will need to feel as polished as the brand. For senior university teams, graduation is a reputation moment as much as an event: leadership, students, families, faculty, and guests all see the standard at once.

Given your senior role, I am keeping the point at the institutional level: graduation is a public reputation moment, not only an event setup.

EGS provides graduation ceremony production across the full physical scope: design, production, installation, on-site management, student registration support, stage setup, seating arrangements, venue branding, LED screen backdrops, lighting and sound system setup, audio-video support, and controlled removal after the ceremony.

EGS has handled this kind of pressure before. Our internal project record includes HCT's UAE-wide graduation season: seven grand ceremonies in 2025 for 4,500 graduates and 13,500 guests, plus eight grand ceremonies in 2024 for 3,500 graduates and 10,000 guests.

You can see our relevant events and graduation work here: https://exhibitgraphicsign.com/events

Would you be open to a short online call this week, or an in-person meeting at your convenience? It would be good to discuss how EGS can support Les Roches Abu Dhabi's next graduation ceremony. If you are not the right person for graduation ceremony production, could you please point me to the right person?

Best Regards,
Masuood-ul-Rasheed

For events and operations teams, the job is to make the room, flow, stage, AV, seating, signage, and rehearsal path feel controlled before guests arrive.

Because your role sits close to events, operations, student affairs, or venue delivery, I am framing this around room readiness, rehearsal flow, supplier coordination, and event-day control.

## Sheet: QA Checklist
| Check | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| Send cadence | Email 1; FU1 after 3 business days; FU2 after 4 more; FU3 after 5 more; FU4 after 7 more; FU5 after 7 more. |
| Stop rules | Stop on reply, pause on bounce, stop after negative reply, and do not send all five follow-ups quickly. |
| Banned phrases | Avoid the user-specified banned wording, unsupported claims, price anchoring, competitor comparisons, and intrusive personal detail. |
| Research wording | Use public wording such as 'I was looking at', 'I came across', or 'public page lists'. Do not imply private monitoring. |
| Proof attribution | Public university pages confirm event context; internal EGS proof confirms EGS production role. |
| Role verification | Where a role is not official-site verified, review the LinkedIn URL or source-export note before sending. |
| Email-source columns | Do not merge Apollo, Hunter, and Lusha emails. Choose the best address manually before upload. |
| Routing language | Always ask the recipient to point to the right person when fit is uncertain. |
| HCT account caution | Coordinate internally before sending to HCT contacts because HCT is already an EGS proof/client context. |
